<p class="note">Inline reduction belongs to the OT script. Quicklook and raw ingestion start when ToltecDB marks the file valid.</p>

<!--
Inline: Tlaloc runs reduce.sh synchronously when the OT script sends a reduce command; the next OT action waits.
Quicklook: the operator queries by obsnum after the script ends, so the sooner it is ready the better.
Ingestion: feeds the portal; it can lag unless quicklook depends on it (it does not, in v3).
-->

<p class="note">2025-10-31, 10 networks. Each pointing obsnum records a Tune scan, then a 60 s Lissajous scan.</p>

Starts: ToltecDB Date/Time; programs: LMTMC ObsPgm/ObsGoal. Ends: toltec0 headers (145605, 145606, 145609),
telescope files (145608, 145610/145611 Lissajous), otherwise estimated from 145606/145609 (evidence note).
Reduce markers inside Tunes use the 145609 block offsets. Script contents: taco_recipes main c9e7364,
observing_scripts/00_detector_setup/10_toltec_detector_init_coarse.lmtot, 12_toltec_tune_3_iters.lmtot,
01_general_scripts/01_standard_pointing.lmtot.
-->

---
# OT nodes drive Tlaloc

| OT node | Tlaloc action, per network |
|---|---|
| `VnaSweep Cal`, `TargSweep Cal` | acquire one sweep, close the file |
| `ToltecRoachVnaReduce`, `ToltecRoachTargReduce` | run `reduce.sh` and wait; read its report |
| `ToltecRoachTargLoadAmpsMode` | select LUT or FILE amplitudes |
| `ToltecRoachTargLoadFromFile` | read the report, upload the tones |
| `Tune Cal` | sweep, reduce, upload, sweep, reduce, close |
| `Lissajous` (pointing) | scan 1 Tune, scan 2 timestream |

<p class="note">Tlaloc runs one manager per network, so the networks reduce in parallel.</p>

<!--
Tlaloc main e967166, src/ToltecRoachManager.cc: reduce_do L1203 (toltec_system, status not checked; then targ_read_file
for VNA/TARG), targ_load_from_file L1275, targ_load L1307, targ_sweep L1623, vna_sweep L1815, targ_read_file L1885,
exec_tune L2347. Cal modes reach Tlaloc through LMT, outside the inspected code.
The Tune scan at the start of each pointing obsnum is recorded (LMTMC ObsPgm Tune, ObsGoal Pointing).
-->

---

Tune ×3 script: (TargSweep Cal, TargReduce, TargLoadFromFile) ×2, Tune Cal.
The inline steps shown are v3's content for every sweep.
-->

<!--
145609 toltec0 headers (ObsStartTime/ObsEndTime) and RecvTime blocks. No gap between scans by design:
the Lissajous does not wait for the Tune quicklook.
reduce.sh row: slowest of the 10 recorded networks started at once (Docker), clip side included, no SSH (slide 9).
v2 block 1 (9.0 s) is within the recorded 9.6 s before the file closes. v3: inline request, tables staged.
Tune QL offsets: Docker, from file close (v2 52.7 s, v3 13.3 s).
Pointing QL: run start 6.8 s (Docker Tune QL), tolteca_reduce preparation 15.2 s (APT matching against the 137389
beammap, model stubs, telescope-coordinate stub), input projection 2.2 s + Citlali 23.6 s. tolteca_reduce.prepare.native_config
rejects reduction_type pointing, so Citlali ran on the driver's prepared inputs from a script
(python/run/pointing-reduction-20261102). Fit values are not validated against v2.
-->

<!--
reduce.sh on the clip binds the request and installs results (taco_recipes v3 kids.run_hook); the Taca worker reduces
(kids.run_worker). Rule from the user [claude 11-02]: TonePrep prepares the tones and TlalocOutput installs
them for every sweep; the native fit provides the model only. In v3 previous, Target/Tune install the fit's f_out.
The inline reduction stages its high-level tables, without block Zarr, as v2 wrote only the kids_find and chan_prop
tables [claude 11-02]; they form one version per acquisition, registered after Valid. Who publishes it is open.
-->

<!--
Docker VM, 12 CPUs: the 10 recorded networks of each block start at once, as each Tlaloc manager calls reduce.sh;
the bar is the slowest network. No SSH in either version. Light segment = clip side: v2 native kids (host-measured,
0.7 s Target/Tune, 2.2 s VNA); v3 hook (2.1–2.2 s, Docker, one at a time) plus native fit (0.7 s, host).
Dark = Taca side: v2 dispatch_tlaloc → dispatch_tolteca_kids; v3 SweepCheck → KidsFind → TonePrep → TlalocOutput,
then the KidsFind and tone tables staged as files (Q1 answer: one version per acquisition, no block Zarr).
The version is published and registered once per acquisition after Valid: raw records 0.35–0.70 s, version 0.10–0.24 s,
registration 0.25–0.50 s (fresh DPDB). Registering it before the raw records fails ("Unknown association target"),
and a Tune reduces block 1 before its file closes; who publishes the version is open.
Saving a version in every inline call instead: 11.2–21.8 s, export waits on the product's version index and 2 of 70
concurrent registrations failed (UNIQUE constraint on actor.uid). VNA varied by ~1.5 s between bursts (one run each).
Source: python/run/inline-full-20261102 (summary.json, burst).
-->

---

<!--
Readiness as in toltecpipe: every known interface row of the acquisition is valid. Dagster's sensor daemon loop has
a 5 s floor (MIN_INTERVAL_LOOP_TIME, 1.6.6 and 1.13.20). One sensor tick records readiness and requests the quicklook
and raw-ingestion runs (tested in Docker). The run waits for inline results with a timeout.
-->

---

<!--
Docker, 13 networks, one run per variant. v2: toltecpipe run end (ql_server answers "running" until then).
v3 previous: kids_quicklook direct mode re-reduces, then a second run registers. v3: the run reads the inline
tables and registers in the same run; plots and feedback are byte-identical to v3 previous.
-->

---
# What each workflow writes

| Workflow | Writes | DPDB |
|---|---|---|
| Inline `reduce.sh` | tone files and model for Tlaloc; KidsFind and tone tables, staged | one version per acquisition, after Valid |
| Quicklook run | `dp_quicklook` version; raw records it read | registers in the same run |
| Raw ingestion | `dp_raw_obs` for every interface; associations | registers on its own |

<p class="note">A DPDB run is not one product: the quicklook run registers the raw records it read, so it does not wait for ingestion.</p>

<!--
Inline version [claude 11-02]: high-level tables only, as v2 wrote kids_find and chan_prop ECSV; no block Zarr.
Measured layout: each call stages its tables; one version per acquisition is published and registered after Valid
(registration needs the raw records). Open: whether the quicklook run or another step publishes it.
Concurrent registration of the same acquisition by quicklook and ingestion is not yet tested.
-->

---
<!-- _class: section-title -->
<!-- _paginate: false -->

<p class="eyebrow">Part 2</p>

# Detector power setup

<p class="note">v3 previous: relative power at a fixed attenuation · v3: detector power in dBm, applied in Tune</p>

<!--
v3 previous (slides 14–19): tolteca/design/detector-setup-spec.md (revision 1.0), sections 2, 5–8, which consolidate
the detector power setup specification (python/run/detector-power-setup-20261022/specification.md, revision 1.1).
v3 (slides 20–22): the power-setting contract agreed in the group chat on 2026-10-27 [user, pasted claude 11-03].
-->

---
# Two settings set each tone's power <span class="tag open">v3 previous</span>

$$
p_i = 10\log_{10}\frac{b_i^2}{\sum_k b_k^2} - A
$$

| Setting | File in `etc/toltecN/` | Written by | Used by |
|---|---|---|---|
| Amplitude $b_i$ per tone | `default_targ_amps.dat` | inline reduction (TonePrep); commit | target loads in FILE mode |
| Amplitude LUT ℓ(f) | `amps_correction_lut.csv` | calibration | VNA loads; target loads in LUT mode |
| Drive attenuation $A$ | `atten_drive.dat` | commit only | `AttensSetDriveFromFile` |

<p class="note">Tone power after the drive attenuator, relative to the total DAC power, which is fixed. Sums run over active DAC bins.</p>

<!--
Detector setup spec §6.3 (power model), §7.3 (fixed attenuation during ordinary tuning), §8.1 (Tlaloc files).
Tlaloc reads default_targ_amps.dat only in FILE mode; in LUT mode it evaluates amps_correction_lut.csv at each RF
tone frequency (Tlaloc main e967166, ToltecRoachManager.cc L686–745). atten_drive.dat is read only by
attens_set_drive_from_file (L3361). A load never changes the attenuation.
Absolute detector-input power needs a DAC calibration and path losses (spec §6.4); the deck uses relative power.
-->

---
# TonePrep: power at a fixed attenuation <span class="tag open">v3 previous</span>

<text x="800.0" y="64.0" text-anchor="start" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" font-size="19" font-weight="700" fill="#17283d">every prepared tone</text>
<text x="800.0" y="100.0" text-anchor="start" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" font-size="18" font-weight="400" fill="#516276">· keeps its probe&#x27;s amplitude</text>
<text x="800.0" y="134.0" text-anchor="start" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" font-size="18" font-weight="400" fill="#516276">· coefficients scaled to ≤ 1</text>
<text x="800.0" y="168.0" text-anchor="start" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" font-size="18" font-weight="400" fill="#516276">· drive attenuation unchanged</text>
<text x="800.0" y="202.0" text-anchor="start" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" font-size="18" font-weight="400" fill="#516276">· phases: random or from file</text>
<text x="760.0" y="266.0" text-anchor="end" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" font-size="15" font-weight="400" fill="#516276">schematic</text>
</svg>

| Prepared tone | Amplitude |
|---|---|
| Matched to one input probe | the probe's amplitude, as in v2 |
| Several tones on one probe | the probe's amplitude by default; optional LUT fill k·ℓ(f) |

<p class="note">With the total power fixed, adding or removing tones shifts the power of every other tone.</p>

<!--
Spec §7.1–7.3; roach-tone-preparation-review-20261030.md. Default policy reproduces v2 exactly (eight production-v2
comparisons: VNA 145480/145605, target 145485/145606, Tune 145483/145609 blocks 0 and 1).
LUT fill [user, 01a15a02 10-23]: k = median over unambiguous probes of 20 log10(a_j / ℓ(f_j)), one global scale.
Ambiguous matches take the LUT fill when enabled [user 10-23]. A common normalization keeps all coefficients ≤ 1.
Attenuation unchanged [user, kids 10-30 19:28]: no hook in the OT sequence applies a new attenuation after a sweep
[user, 01a15a02 10-23 18:20].
Phases: random or file (v2). Optional crest-factor search at fixed amplitudes: lower crest factor at a fixed waveform
peak raises every tone's power (0.71 dB on a 1,000-tone comb, 32 candidates); it is reported, not compensated.
In LUT mode (init and tune scripts) Tlaloc ignores the prepared amplitudes and evaluates the LUT at each load.
-->

---

<p class="note"><code>21_…</code>: Tune, then <code>TargSweep</code> at A₀−6 … A₀+6 dB on a fixed comb, then <code>DriveFitReduce</code>. Each tone takes the first attenuation where a reaches 0.2. Network 0: 506 of 632 accepted.</p>

<!--
Recorded 2025-10-31: obsnum 145484, subobservations 1–7 (4–16 dB), network 0, 632 channels.
Spec §2.7 (v3 sequence) and §5.4 (selection). A power point counts when its fit succeeded and −0.2 ≤ a ≤ 1.5;
the crossing of a_ref = 0.2 is interpolated linearly in power between the first bracketing pair; otherwise the
endpoint is taken and flagged. Channel 450: a = 0.183 at 10 dB, 0.326 at 8 dB → 9.76 dB.
The spec's worked channel 596 excludes its 6 dB point (a = −0.264) and selects 5.49 dB.
v3 removes the per-step TargReduce of the site script: the comb stays fixed and the prepared report is untouched.
Fit: analytic Jacobian, Levenberg–Marquardt, 200 evaluations per solve; stable to 1e-12 dB under 1e-15 I/Q scaling,
where the v2 finite-difference fit moved channel 596 by 4.5 dB (spec §6.4 of the power spec).
Product: dp_drivefit (pinned realization; channel summary and per-power arrays).
-->

---

$$
t_i = r_{m(i)}\,10^{-A_i/10},\qquad b_i=\sqrt{t_i/t_{\max}},\qquad A_\star=-10\log_{10}\textstyle\sum_i t_i
$$

<p class="note">Network 0, fit 145484 → target 145485: A⋆ = 8.35 dB, written 8.25 dB (0.25 dB steps, rounded down).</p>

<!--
Spec §6. Commit matches the target's input probes to the fit by frequency (DTW, ≤ 10 MHz shift); r is the
matched reference tone's fraction of the full reference comb power. Rejected tones take the median (8.79 dB);
selections below the 10th percentile (6.0 dB) are raised to it. The amplitudes replace the input amplitudes;
they are not multipliers. Every tone ends 0.098 dB above its request (quantization).
v2 normalized only the largest amplitude: on the same data it writes 6.25 dB, +2.10 dB on every tone
(networks 7 and 11: +0.98 and +0.79 dB). drive-fit-normalization-20261021.md, Table 2.
Writes default_targ_amps.dat and atten_drive.dat after checking targ_freqs.dat against the target's comb
(LO, order, 488.28125 Hz bins). Product: dp_drive_fit_commit, referencing the pinned fit.
-->

<!--
Spec §2.3–2.9. User [01a15a02 10-20]: every VNA sweep starts a new calibration group from a fixed 1000-tone comb with
a LUT for per-tone power; commit reuses a previous drive fit after a VNA sweep instead of redoing it, and prepares
the Tlaloc files so that the next sweep acts as if a drive fit had just run.
Fresh setup (21_…): LUT Tune; series; DriveFitReduce; load the pending placement; new TargSweep, not reduced;
DriveFitCommit; FILE; TargLoadFromFile; AttensSetDriveFromFile; verification Tune (its first sweep records the
committed state). Reuse (13_…): VNA and refinements in LUT mode at the kept attenuation; new unreduced TargSweep;
commit with a pinned saved fit; FILE reload; apply attenuation; Tune.
After commit, inline reductions carry the committed amplitudes to new placements at the committed attenuation.
-->

---
# Power setup: v2 → v3 previous

| Step | v2 site scripts | v3 previous |
|---|---|---|
| Power series | reduce after each sweep | no reduction; comb and report unchanged |
| Commit input | last reduced sweep; reload list can differ | new unreduced sweep; reload list checked |
| After a fresh commit | FILE selected, no reload | FILE reload, attenuation, verification Tune |
| Reuse of a saved fit | attenuation not applied | attenuation applied |
| Common attenuation | from the largest amplitude: +2.1 dB (nw0) | from the summed power: +0.10 dB |
| Saved fit | `_adrv.ecsv` nearest in time | pinned `dp_drivefit` product |

<p class="note">The v3 previous OT profiles are authored and checked offline; they have not run at the site.</p>

<!--
Spec §2.5–2.6 (gaps of the inspected site scripts) and §2.9; drive-fit-normalization-20261021.md.
v2 commit loads the drive-fit table nearest in time (taco_recipes dispatch_drivefit.py _load_drivefit_data).
Recorded 2025-10-31 network 0: 145485 and 145486 run in FILE mode at 7.25 dB, but their tone amplitudes stay
in the LUT range (max 0.469); a committed amplitude file peaks at 1. Production capture analysis (January)
found commit amplitude files overwritten by the next sweep reduction before the FILE upload
(run/drive-fit-production-investigation-20261026).
The dispatcher must also report per-network failures to OT; the v2 shell wrapper returns success unconditionally.
The v3 reduce.sh hook (taco_recipes kids.py) does not route drivefit_reduce or drivefit_commit yet; the
drive_fit and drive_fit_commit workflows run standalone (spec §9.2 requires the routing).
-->

<!--
Agreed contract [user, group chat 2026-10-27, pasted to claude 11-03], replacing the relative-dB, fixed-attenuation
contract of the previous slides:
- The drive-fit artifact anchors every detector power setting in dBm; the KIDs reduction (TonePrep) prepares the tone
  power settings passed to Tlaloc so that the desired powers are realized.
- The ROACH power-chain config (v2 tolteca_kids roach_tone_power.py: gains and attenuations of each readout stage,
  forward and backward per-stage power in physical units per sweep) is versioned and persisted, because absolute
  power depends on it. The v2 drive fit did not use it; it worked in relative dB on the drive-attenuation scale.
- Tlaloc interface: the tone configuration (frequency, amplitude, phase, mask, gain, attenuation) is one unit with one
  identifier, consumed directly like the model report ECSV; Tlaloc validates it instead of renormalizing; acquisition
  metadata records applied gain, DAC peaks, digital power, attenuation readback and the identifier; missing amplitude
  or phase input is rejected, not replaced by unity amplitudes or random phases.
- A drive fit need not be repeated when tones change merely to correct normalization.
The single tone configuration and validation-only normalization simplify the handling; the Tune change on the next
slide is the minimal change that supports the workflow [user 10-27].
-->

<p class="note">Today's Tune uploads only the comb (slide 6). A failed application fails the Tune.</p>

<!--
Agreed [user 10-27]: this coordinated application is the minimal Tlaloc change that supports the workflow.
Sequence: block 0 sweep and reduction identify and recenter resonances; TonePrep prepares and validates the comb, its
gain and the associated attenuation; Tlaloc applies them, verifies the readback and allows settling; block 1 sweep and
reduction give the model for the next scan. The two-block Tune and its calibration of the next scan are unchanged.
The hardware writes may not be simultaneous: ordering and partial-failure behavior are to be defined with Kamal so an
intermediate state cannot overdrive detectors. Tlaloc already has an attenuation-setting command; the missing piece is
its coordination with Tune's intermediate upload. Analog limits and deployment details still need confirmation.
-->

---
# Power settings: v2, v3 previous, v3

| Item | v2 | v3 previous | v3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| Power target | attenuation, relative dB | relative to total DAC power | detector power in dBm |
| Drive attenuation | set by commit | set by commit; fixed while tuning | prepared each Tune, applied with the comb |
| When tones change | other tones' power shifts | other tones' power shifts | amplitudes and attenuation re-solved |
| Normalization | Tlaloc divides by the peak | same | Tlaloc validates the requested gain |
| Tlaloc input | tone files + attenuation file | same | one tone configuration |
| Recorded | tone settings, attenuation | same | applied gain, DAC peaks, readback |

<p class="note">v3 agreed 2026-10-27. It needs the Tlaloc Tune change first; none of it is implemented yet.</p>

<!--
v2 and v3 previous: detector setup spec §6–§7 (fixed-total model, fixed attenuation during ordinary tuning,
attenuation applied only by AttensSetDriveFromFile); Tlaloc main e967166 normalizes the waveform by its complex peak,
which cancels any uniform gain in the amplitudes (spec §6.4). TonePrep's current implementation keeps the attenuation
unchanged [kids 10-30 19:28]. v3: the agreed contract [user 10-27]; detector-setup-spec.md §7.3 and the TonePrep
fixed-attenuation rule still describe v3 previous.
-->
